 from langchain_core.tools import tool
@@ -10,6 +11,36 @@ from logging_config import get_logger
 
 logger = get_logger(__name__)
 
+# One or more backslashes before an escape letter, or a bare run of them.
+_ESCAPED_BREAK_RE = re.compile(r"\\+([nt])")
+_BACKSLASH_RUN_RE = re.compile(r"\\{2,}")
+_OUTLINE_LINE_RE = re.compile(r"^\d+\.\s")
+_OUTLINE_NAME_RE = re.compile(r"outline", re.IGNORECASE)
+
+
+def _holds_outline(path: str) -> bool:
+    """Whether a file already holds a create_outline result rather than a document."""
+    try:
+        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            return bool(_OUTLINE_LINE_RE.match(f.readline()))
+    except OSError:
+        return False
+
+
+def _unescape(text: str) -> str:
+    r"""Turn the model's escape sequences back into the characters it meant.
+
+    The model writes a line break inside a JSON tool argument as the two
+    characters \ and n, and this server's constrained decoder hands them over
+    verbatim -- so a document arrives with a literal "\n" everywhere its lines
+    should break. The same habit degenerates into long backslash runs, which are
+    always a separator the model failed to encode. Fixing it here keeps every
+    tool that writes a file honest, rather than asking the model to escape
+    correctly on a decoder that will not let it.
+    """
+    text = _ESCAPED_BREAK_RE.sub(lambda m: "\n" if m.group(1) == "n" else "\t", text)
+    return _BACKSLASH_RUN_RE.sub("\n", text)
+
All nine files are in yt-tutorial/. Nothing in orchestrator/, docs/, Build-Guide.md, or cli-tools.json changed — so the plan's structural foundations hold, but the commit produced several transferable lessons that materially change the design.

Part 1 — What changed in ef6b559
"Enhance LLM client and invoker with timeout handling and frequency penalty" — 9 files, +668/−107

config.py (+28) — four new knobs
Constant	Value	Why
LLM_FREQUENCY_PENALTY	0.4	Near-greedy decoding loops inside a grammar-constrained tool argument; escaping doubles each pass until max_tokens dies mid-string → server rejects with "EOF while parsing a string" after ~2min. Temperature stays low so routing keeps determinism.
WORKER_DEADLINE_SECONDS	180	Wall-clock ceiling on a whole worker turn
TOOL_RESULT_SALVAGE_CHARS	700	Cap on forwarded raw tool output
WORKER_ERROR_REPORT_CHARS	300	Provider errors quote the whole rejected generation
Also: TOOL_CALL_RUN_LIMIT's semantics changed (now blocks further calls rather than ending the run); STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_METHOD moved 49 → config.py:56.

llm/invoker.py (+70) — the headline addition
run_with_deadline(fn, *, timeout, name) + DeadlineExceededError. Enforces wall-clock time, replacing the ThreadPoolExecutor in _invoke_once.
Uses a daemon thread, not an executor — concurrent.futures registers an atexit hook joining its non-daemon threads, and shutdown(wait=False) doesn't opt out, so an abandoned call kept the interpreter alive after the run printed its summary.
Copies contextvars so records logged from the thread keep the run id.
llm/clients.py (+13)
frequency_penalty and timeout now set on the client itself, for both custom and Groq. Worker calls go through create_agent and never reach invoker.py — without this the SDK's 600s default applied.

teams/supervisor.py (+143) — largest behavioral change
tool_call_limit: exit_behavior "end" → "continue" — the worker keeps its turn to report after exhausting the budget.
New single_call_limit(tool_name) — write_document/create_outline replace the whole file, so a second call destroys the first, and the last call is reliably the thinnest.
New run_worker() — catches deadline + any exception and returns a report: "A supervisor can route around a worker that failed; it cannot route around a traceback."
agent_report() rewritten — searches backwards for the last real AI message, appends tool calls as evidence ("prose is only a claim").
New _tool_results() — salvages raw tool output when the worker produced no prose; skips error-status results.
teams/orchestrator.py (+62)
New team_report(result, sent) — folds back every new message, not just the closing one.
New _call_team() — passes the full history into a team (previously only messages[-1]), so a re-invoked team isn't handed its own prior report and the writing team actually sees the original request.
Remaining
research.py (+12) / writing.py (+26) — workers switched to run_worker; doc_writer gains single_call_limit("write_document"). tools/documents.py (+79) — _unescape() converts the decoder's literal \n into real newlines, _holds_outline() distinguishes outline from document. prompts.py (+342) — role and decision wording.

Worth flagging: this is substantial hardening of a sandbox the README calls "due for deletion when Stage 1 begins," and none of these findings are in Bugs.md or Decisions.md yet.

Part 2 — Recreated plan
Verdict: unchanged — possible, and still Stage 6 work
maestro delegate 0.5.61 supplies --to codex|claude, --role, --mode, --async, --resume, status/output/cancel/message (verified against the installed binary). Build-Guide.md:201 still places the Agent Router at Stage 6; the deterministic tier is still what's buildable now.

Constraints — 5 carried forward, 6 new from this commit
Carried forward (all re-verified): two tiers not three peers · claude.enabled:false · never pass --role without --to (maestro's --to > --role > first-enabled would silently land planning work on codex) · maestro not on PATH · no silent downgrade to local for repo-write tasks.

New, from ef6b559:

N1 — maestro delegate --timeout has the exact blind spot the sandbox just fixed. It's documented as a stale-stream timeout: "force-terminate the CLI after this much silence." That is the same class as httpx's per-read timeout, and config.py:88 records why it's insufficient — "a server that holds the socket open while it generates never trips it; one turn ran 388s before the server answered at all." A Codex or Claude CLI streaming tokens while making no progress will never trip --timeout. The dispatcher needs its own wall-clock deadline over the subprocess, structurally the same as run_with_deadline. This is the single most important addition to the plan.

N2 — Two distinct failure outcomes, not one. My earlier plan collapsed both into unroutable/exit 1. run_worker's lesson splits them:

Outcome	When	Result
unroutable	Pre-dispatch: no target survives the gates	exit 1, reason recorded
dispatch_failed	Post-dispatch: agent errored, timed out, or overran deadline	Structured report the router can re-route on — never an exception
N3 — A bound must cut work, not the report. exit_behavior="continue". Stage 6's per-step attempt cap must leave the agent a turn to say what it did.

N4 — Capture --full, act on the final reply. maestro delegate output <id> returns the final reply only; --full returns all entries. That default is precisely the collapse team_report/agent_report were just rewritten to stop. The router should persist --full for the durable record even when it acts on the summary.

N5 — Re-dispatching a write step is destructive. single_call_limit exists because a second whole-file write destroys the first. Stage 6's attempt cap can't treat retries as free.

N6 — Enforce on every path to the resource. The timeout is now applied twice because create_agent bypassed invoker.py. If the router can reach an agent via both the maestro CLI and the MCP channel, the deadline and budget must sit on both.

Design
Unchanged in shape: a pure function (RouteRequest, Capabilities) → RouteDecision, with all environment probing hoisted into a separately-gathered Capabilities snapshot so the decision is unit-testable with no subprocess, endpoint, or Mongo.


RouteRequest:   task_text, role|None, needs_repo_write, needs_filesystem,
                needs_session_resume, explicit_to|None
Capabilities:   per-target {enabled, binary_resolved, reachable, detail}
RouteDecision:  target, tier, reason[], maestro_argv|None,
                deadline_seconds, confidence, failure|None      ← deadline + failure are new
Precedence (unchanged): explicit --to → capability gate → availability gate → role table → ambiguous fallback → unroutable.

Role table, from Architecture.md:58: plan/architect/design/review → claude (analysis); implement/fix/refactor/test → codex (write); classify/summarize/extract/rank → local.

Files: one module orchestrator/router.py, matching preflight.py's conventions (stdlib-only, frozen dataclasses, main() -> int). Requires lifting _load_cli_tools, _resolve_executable, _read_json into a shared helper both import. Must not import from yt-tutorial/ (README:26 — the tracks share no code).

New in Phase A because of N1: a run_with_deadline-equivalent for subprocesses. It is not a port — the sandbox version bounds an in-process call it cannot cancel, whereas a subprocess can actually be killed, so this one terminates the process group and is simpler. The transferable part is the reasoning, not the code.

Phasing
Phase A — now. Deterministic router + probe + CLI + wall-clock dispatch deadline (N1) + the two-outcome failure model (N2). Unit tests over the pure function. Zero new dependencies.
Phase B — ambiguous-case LLM fallback. Needs an OpenAI-compatible client in orchestrator/ plus the tgi_compat shim (ADR-010) and function_calling structured output (config.py:56). This commit adds a prerequisite: structured-output routing runs inside a grammar-constrained tool argument — the exact site of the repetition loop — so frequency_penalty ≈ 0.4 with low temperature is a requirement of this tier, not a tuning detail.
Phase C — Stage 5/6. Ledger state, headroom reservation, switching cost, attempt cap that escalates to a different agent — now constrained by N3 (leave the reporting turn) and N5 (retries of writes are destructive).
One framing note, unchanged
Under maestro delegate, Codex and Claude Code are workers, not supervisors — the supervisor role stays in the orchestrator process. If Claude Code spawns its own subagents internally, that nesting is opaque to the router, which matters for Stage 5 attribution.

Nothing written or changed. Want me to build Phase A?

IN
claude --help 2>&1 | Out-String

OUT
Usage: claude [options] [command] [prompt]

Claude Code - starts an interactive session by default, use -p/--print for
non-interactive output

Arguments:
  prompt                                Your prompt

Options:
  --add-dir <directories...>            Additional directories to allow tool
                                        access to
  --agent <agent>                       Agent for the current session. Overrides
                                        the 'agent' setting.
  --agents <json>                       JSON object defining custom agents (e.g.
                                        '{"reviewer": {"description": "Reviews
                                        code", "prompt": "You are a code
                                        reviewer"}}')
  --allow-dangerously-skip-permissions  Enable bypassing all permission checks
                                        as an option, without it being enabled
                                        by default. Recommended only for
                                        sandboxes with no internet access.
  --allowedTools, --allowed-tools <tools...>
      Comma or space-separated list of tool names to allow (e.g. "Bash(git *)
      Edit")
  --append-system-prompt <prompt>       Append a system prompt to the default
                                        system prompt
  --ax-screen-reader                    Render screen-reader friendly output
                                        (flat text, no decorative borders or
                                        animations).
  --bg, --background                    Start the session as a background agent
                                        and return immediately (manage with
                                        `claude agents`)
  --bare                                Minimal mode: skip hooks, LSP, plugin
                                        sync, attribution, auto-memory,
                                        background prefetches, keychain reads,
                                        and CLAUDE.md auto-discovery. Sets
                                        CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE=1. Anthropic auth is
                                        strictly ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or
                                        apiKeyHelper via --settings (OAuth and
                                        keychain are never read). 3P providers
                                        (Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry) use their own
                                        credentials. Skills still resolve via
                                        /skill-name. Explicitly provide context
                                        via: --system-prompt[-file],
                                        --append-system-prompt[-file], --add-dir
                                        (CLAUDE.md dirs), --mcp-config,
                                        --settings, --agents, --plugin-dir.
  --betas <betas...>                    Beta headers to include in API requests
                                        (API key users only)
  --brief                               Enable SendUserMessage tool for
                                        agent-to-user communication
  --chrome                              Enable Claude in Chrome integration
  -c, --continue                        Continue the most recent conversation in
                                        the current directory
  --dangerously-skip-permissions        Bypass all permission checks.
                                        Recommended only for sandboxes with no
                                        internet access.
  -d, --debug [filter]                  Enable debug mode with optional category
                                        filtering (e.g., "api,hooks" or
                                        "!1p,!file")
  --debug-file <path>                   Write debug logs to a specific file path
                                        (implicitly enables debug mode)
  --disable-slash-commands              Disable all skills
  --disallowedTools, --disallowed-tools <tools...>
      Comma or space-separated list of tool names to deny (e.g. "Bash(git *)
      Edit")
  --effort <level>                      Effort level for the current session
                                        (low, medium, high, xhigh, max)
  --exclude-dynamic-system-prompt-sections
      Move per-machine sections (cwd, env info, memory paths, git status) from
      the system prompt into the first user message. Improves cross-user
      prompt-cache reuse. Only applies with the default system prompt (ignored
      with --system-prompt). (default: false)
  --fallback-model <model>              Enable automatic fallback to specified
                                        model(s) when the default model is
                                        overloaded or not available. Accepts a
                                        comma-separated list to try each in
                                        order. Re-tries the primary at the start
                                        of each user turn. (only works with
                                        --print)
  --file <specs...>                     File resources to download at startup.
                                        Format: file_id:relative_path (e.g.,
                                        --file file_abc:doc.txt
                                        file_def:img.png)
  --fork-session                        When resuming, create a new session ID
                                        instead of reusing the original (use
                                        with --resume or --continue)
  --forward-subagent-text               Forward subagent text and thinking
                                        blocks as assistant/user messages with
                                        parent_tool_use_id set (only works with
                                        --print and --output-format=stream-json)
  --from-pr [value]                     Resume a session linked to a PR by PR
                                        number/URL, or open interactive picker
                                        with optional search term
  -h, --help                            Display help for command
  --ide                                 Automatically connect to IDE on startup
                                        if exactly one valid IDE is available
  --include-hook-events                 Include all hook lifecycle events in the
                                        output stream (only works with
                                        --output-format=stream-json)
  --include-partial-messages            Include partial message chunks as they
                                        arrive (only works with --print and
                                        --output-format=stream-json)
  --input-format <format>               Input format (only works with --print):
                                        "text" (default), or "stream-json"
                                        (realtime streaming input) (choices:
                                        "text", "stream-json")
  --json-schema <schema>                JSON Schema for structured output
                                        validation. Example:
                                        {"type":"object","properties":{"name":{"type":"string"}},"required":["name"]}
  --max-budget-usd <amount>             Maximum dollar amount to spend on API
                                        calls (only works with --print)
  --mcp-config <configs...>             Load MCP servers from JSON files or
                                        strings (space-separated)
  --model <model>                       Model for the current session. Provide
                                        an alias for the latest model (e.g.
                                        'fable', 'opus', or 'sonnet') or a
                                        model's full name (e.g.
                                        'claude-fable-5').
  -n, --name <name>                     Set a display name for this session
                                        (shown in the prompt box, /resume
                                        picker, and terminal title)
  --no-chrome                           Disable Claude in Chrome integration
  --no-session-persistence              Disable session persistence - sessions
                                        will not be saved to disk and cannot be
                                        resumed (only works with --print)
  --output-format <format>              Output format (only works with --print):
                                        "text" (default), "json" (single
                                        result), or "stream-json" (realtime
                                        streaming) (choices: "text", "json",
                                        "stream-json")
  --permission-mode <mode>              Permission mode to use for the session
                                        (choices: "acceptEdits", "auto",
                                        "bypassPermissions", "manual",
                                        "dontAsk", "plan")
  --plugin-dir <path>                   Load a plugin from a directory or .zip
                                        for this session only (repeatable:
                                        --plugin-dir A --plugin-dir B.zip)
                                        (default: [])
  --plugin-url <url>                    Fetch a plugin .zip from a URL for this
                                        session only (repeatable: --plugin-url A
                                        --plugin-url B) (default: [])
  -p, --print                           Print response and exit (useful for
                                        pipes). Note: The workspace trust dialog
                                        is skipped when Claude is run in
                                        non-interactive mode (via -p, or when
                                        stdout is not a TTY, e.g. piped or
                                        redirected output). Only use this in
                                        directories you trust. Settings files
                                        that fail validation are silently
                                        ignored in this mode (no error dialog is
                                        shown).
  --prompt-suggestions [value]          Enable prompt suggestions. In print/SDK
                                        mode, emits a prompt_suggestion message
                                        after each turn with a predicted next
                                        user prompt (choices: "true", "false",
                                        "1", "0", "yes", "no", "on", "off",
                                        preset: "true")
  --remote-control [name]               Start an interactive session with Remote
                                        Control enabled (optionally named)
  --remote-control-session-name-prefix <prefix>
      Prefix for auto-generated Remote Control session names (default: hostname)
  --replay-user-messages                Re-emit user messages from stdin back on
                                        stdout for acknowledgment (only works
                                        with --input-format=stream-json and
                                        --output-format=stream-json)
  -r, --resume [value]                  Resume a conversation by session ID, or
                                        open interactive picker with optional
                                        search term
  --safe-mode                           Start with all customizations
                                        (CLAUDE.md, skills, plugins, hooks, MCP
                                        servers, custom commands and agents,
                                        output styles, workflows, custom themes,
                                        keybindings, and more) disabled — useful
                                        for troubleshooting a broken
                                        configuration. Admin-managed (policy)
                                        settings still apply. Auth, model
                                        selection, built-in tools, and
                                        permissions work normally. Sets
                                        CLAUDE_CODE_SAFE_MODE=1.
  --session-id <uuid>                   Use a specific session ID for the
                                        conversation (must be a valid UUID)
  --setting-sources <sources>           Comma-separated list of setting sources
                                        to load (user, project, local).
  --settings <file-or-json>             Path to a settings JSON file or a JSON
                                        string to load additional settings from
  --strict-mcp-config                   Only use MCP servers from --mcp-config,
                                        ignoring all other MCP configurations
  --system-prompt <prompt>              System prompt to use for the session
  --tmux                                Create a tmux session for the worktree
                                        (requires --worktree). Uses iTerm2
                                        native panes when available; use
                                        --tmux=classic for traditional tmux.
  --tools <tools...>                    Specify the list of available tools from
                                        the built-in set. Use "" to disable all
                                        tools, "default" to use all tools, or
                                        specify tool names (e.g.
                                        "Bash,Edit,Read").
  --verbose                             Override verbose mode setting from
                                        config
  -v, --version                         Output the version number
  -w, --worktree [name]                 Create a new git worktree for this
                                        session (optionally specify a name)

Commands:
  agents [options]                      Manage background agents
  auth                                  Manage authentication
  auto-mode                             Inspect or reset auto mode classifier
                                        configuration
  doctor                                Check the health of your Claude Code
                                        installation. Reads settings files in
                                        the current directory without a trust
                                        prompt. For a full checkup that can also

  gateway [options]                     Run the enterprise auth/telemetry
                                        gateway
  install [options] [target]            Install Claude Code native build. Use

  mcp                                   Configure and manage MCP servers

import os, json
os.environ["USE_CLAUDE_CODE_AGENTS"] = "true"

import claude_agents
from claude_agents.agents import CLAUDE_AGENTS, _tools_note
from claude_agents.cli import _build_argv

from claude_agents import prompts as framing

cfg = mcp_config_json(spec.custom_tools)
print("mcp config:", cfg[:300])
print()
argv = _build_argv(
    system_prompt="<SYS>", builtin_tools=(), mcp_tools=spec.custom_tools,
    mcp_config=cfg, json_schema=None,
)
print("argv:", [a if len(a) < 90 else a[:87] + "..." for a in argv])
print()
print("--- tools note (custom) ---")
print(_tools_note(spec)[:400])
PY

Bash Live test of Claude Code router and doc_writer with custom MCP tools
IN
cd "c:/Users/LOQ/Desktop/Projects/Bhai-To-Bhai/yt-tutorial" && rm -f temp/_cc_test.txt && ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe - <<'PY' 2>&1 | tail -40
import os
os.environ["USE_CLAUDE_CODE_AGENTS"] = "true"
os.environ["CLAUDE_CODE_SEND_CUSTOM_TOOLS"] = "true"

from logging_config import setup_logging, set_request_id
setup_logging(app_name="cc_probe"); set_request_id("probe")

from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage
from claude_agents.agents import make_claude_worker_node
from claude_agents.router import make_claude_supervisor_node
from config import WRITING_TEAM_MEMBERS
from prompts import WRITING_TEAM_SCOPE

state = {"messages": [
    HumanMessage(content="Research 2 open source grammar checkers and compile them into a file named '_cc_test.txt'."),
    HumanMessage(content="1. LanguageTool -- open-source grammar and style checker, LGPL licensed [https://languagetool.org]\n2. Vale -- open-source prose linter, MIT licensed [https://vale.sh]\nStill missing: nothing", name="research_team"),
]}

sup = make_claude_supervisor_node(WRITING_TEAM_MEMBERS, scope=WRITING_TEAM_SCOPE)
cmd = sup(state)
print("ROUTER ->", cmd.goto, cmd.update)

node = make_claude_worker_node("doc_writer")
cmd = node(state)
print("WORKER ->", cmd.goto)
print("REPORT:", cmd.update["messages"][0].content[:400])
PY

Bash Read PythonREPL implementation

cd "c:/Users/LOQ/Desktop/Projects/Bhai-To-Bhai/yt-tutorial" && find .venv/Lib/site-packages/langchain_experimental -name "python.py" -path "*utilities*" | head -1 | xargs cat

OUT
import functools
import logging
import multiprocessing
import re
import sys
from io import StringIO
from typing import Dict, Optional

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=None)
def warn_once() -> None:
    """Warn once about the dangers of PythonREPL."""
    logger.warning("Python REPL can execute arbitrary code. Use with caution.")


class PythonREPL(BaseModel):
    """Simulates a standalone Python REPL."""

    globals: Optional[Dict] = Field(default_factory=dict, alias="_globals")  # type: ignore[arg-type]
    locals: Optional[Dict] = Field(default_factory=dict, alias="_locals")  # type: ignore[arg-type]

    @staticmethod
    def sanitize_input(query: str) -> str:
        """Sanitize input to the python REPL.

        Remove whitespace, backtick & python
        (if llm mistakes python console as terminal)

        Args:
            query: The query to sanitize

        Returns:
            str: The sanitized query
        """
        query = re.sub(r"^(\s|`)*(?i:python)?\s*", "", query)
        query = re.sub(r"(\s|`)*$", "", query)
        return query

    @classmethod
    def worker(
        cls,
        command: str,
        globals: Optional[Dict],
        locals: Optional[Dict],
        queue: multiprocessing.Queue,
    ) -> None:
        old_stdout = sys.stdout
        sys.stdout = mystdout = StringIO()
        try:
            cleaned_command = cls.sanitize_input(command)
            exec(cleaned_command, globals, locals)
            sys.stdout = old_stdout
            queue.put(mystdout.getvalue())
        except Exception as e:
            sys.stdout = old_stdout
            queue.put(repr(e))

    def run(self, command: str, timeout: Optional[int] = None) -> str:
        """Run command with own globals/locals and returns anything printed.
        Timeout after the specified number of seconds."""

        # Warn against dangers of PythonREPL
        warn_once()

        queue: multiprocessing.Queue = multiprocessing.Queue()

        # Only use multiprocessing if we are enforcing a timeout
        if timeout is not None:
            # create a Process
            p = multiprocessing.Process(
                target=self.worker, args=(command, self.globals, self.locals, queue)
            )

            # start it
            p.start()

            # wait for the process to finish or kill it after timeout seconds
            p.join(timeout)

            if p.is_alive():
                p.terminate()
                return "Execution timed out"
        else:
            self.worker(command, self.globals, self.locals, queue)
        # get the result from the worker function
        return queue.get()

Open Source Tools for Building a Document Writing Assistant

1. Writing Tools (theJayTea/WritingTools)
A completely free and open-source, system-wide grammar and writing assistant. Supports local LLMs (via Ollama) as well as cloud LLMs (Gemini, ChatGPT, Mistral) through an OpenAI-API-compatible interface.
Source: https://github.com/theJayTea/WritingTools

2. Ghostwriter
A distraction-free Markdown editor licensed under GPL v3. Supports exporting documents to HTML, Word, ODT, PDF, and EPUB.
Source: https://itsfoss.com/open-source-tools-writers

3. GemType (riponcm/GemType)
A free, open-source Grammarly alternative for Chrome and Safari. Provides AI-powered grammar checking and text rewriting, powered by the user's own Gemini API key.
Source: https://github.com/topics/ai-writing-assistant

4. Harper
A local-first, open-source grammar checker. A newer project developed by an Automattic developer.
Source: https://itsfoss.com/harper-grammar-checker

5. Manubot
A free, open-source authoring platform built on a Git repository. Lets authors write manuscripts in Markdown and convert them to HTML, PDF, or DOC formats.
Source: https://guides.ucsf.edu/writingpublishing/writingtools

Read c:\Users\LOQ\Desktop\Projects\Bhai-To-Bhai\yt-tutorial\claude_agents\agents.py (lines 109-168)
Done. Here's what's in place.

What was built
Six files in yt-tutorial/claude_agents/, plus a config section and the wiring:

File	Role
router.py	Supervisor that asks Claude Code where to route
agents.py	search, doc_writer, note_taker, chart_generator
cli.py	The single place the CLI is invoked; all cost/timing/error logging
mcp_bridge.py	Serves your tools/ to Claude Code over MCP
prompts.py	Framing only — no rewritten roles — plus transcript rendering
The prompts are the same ones. Each agent imports its brief verbatim from prompts.py (SEARCH_AGENT, DOC_WRITER_AGENT, …) and the router assembles SUPERVISOR_BASE + scope + SUPERVISOR_TERMINATION_RULES exactly as teams/supervisor.py:184-187 does. The only added text is framing: you're in a pipeline, your reply is the report, and here's what your brief's tool names map to.

The two switches are in config.py:139-176, env-driven like everything else:


USE_CLAUDE_CODE_AGENTS=true          # Claude Code agents vs. the current ones
CLAUDE_CODE_SEND_CUSTOM_TOOLS=true   # your tools/ over MCP vs. Claude Code's own
Plus CLAUDE_CODE_MODEL (defaults sonnet — the CLI's own default is Opus at ~10× the cost), CLAUDE_CODE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS, CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_BUDGET_USD.

Verified
Full main.py run with the flag on: 5 steps, 94s, $1.05 across 9 CLI calls, produced a grounded research_results.txt from real Tavily results — including the research → writing → FINISH routing the 8B supervisor gets wrong. All four workers tested in both tool modes; the graph is byte-identical either way.

Three real bugs found while testing, all fixed
MCP tools silently absent. Claude Code connects --mcp-config servers in the background and doesn't wait. The bridge takes ~3s to import langchain, so doc_writer got zero tools and reported writing a file it had no way to write. Fixed with alwaysLoad: true, which puts the connect on the blocking path.
Charts written nowhere. Claude Code's own system prompt tells it to use a scratchpad directory, and that beat the brief's abstract "workspace directory" — the chart went to AppData\Local\Temp. The system prompt now names the real workspace path and overrides that instruction.
Tool work invisible. The MCP server is a separate process and Claude Code keeps its stderr, so nothing your tools logged reached the run log. The bridge now attaches to the run's own debug file (that's how the chart bug got diagnosed).
Two things to know
web_scraper has no Claude Code counterpart — you listed router, search, doc_writer, note_taker, chart_generator, so that's what I built. It keeps running on the LangChain agent in either mode; research.py:62-64 says so. Say the word and I'll add it.
The router keeps one piece of logic: the max_worker_reports repeat guard from supervisor.py:225-235. Everything else is prompt-and-delegate, but without that guard a looping supervisor bills a paid CLI call every turn until the recursion limit.
The regression run on the original path completed cleanly but stopped before the writing team — that's the pre-existing 8B early-FINISH your TOP_LEVEL_SCOPE comments describe, not a change from this work; the diff shows those functions are renamed, not modified.

Tasks to implement:
Inside yt-tutorial/temp_agents/ folder,
- Create a temp_supervisor, agent_A, and agent_B (claude code based)
- Create very small simple prompts and do not reuse the old ones.
- temp_supervisor: Starts Agent A and Agent B simultaneously, then waits for both.
- Agent A is supposed to write "Hello World!" in the yt-tutorial/temp_agents/file_A.txt file.
- Agent B is supposed to write "Hi World!" in the yt-tutorial/temp_agents/file_B.txt file.
- Agent A can be codex based or claude code based, depending on a variable set on the config.py file
- Agent B can be codex based or claude code based, depending on a variable set on the config.py file

Done and working in both directions.

What's in yt-tutorial/temp_agents/
File	Role
supervisor.py	temp_supervisor() — submits both agents, then collects both results
agents.py	AGENT_A / AGENT_B — name, backend, prompt, target file
runners.py	run_claude and run_codex, dispatched by backend name
prompts.py	Three new prompts, ~4 lines each. Nothing reused
main.py	python -m temp_agents.main
Standalone — it shares nothing with teams/ or claude_agents/ beyond config.py and the logger.

The switch is config.py:153-165:


TEMP_AGENT_A_BACKEND = "claude"   # or "codex"
TEMP_AGENT_B_BACKEND = "codex"    # or "claude"
I defaulted them to one each so a first run exercises both paths. Env vars override.

Simultaneity is in supervisor.py:38-40 — both submit() calls complete before any .result(), so the two CLIs overlap. Visible in the swapped run: agent_B finished at 18:29:11 while agent_A ran until 18:29:42.

Verified both ways
Run	agent_A	agent_B	file_A.txt	file_B.txt
default	claude	codex	Hello World!	Hi World!
swapped	codex	claude	Hello World!	Hi World!
Byte-checked with xxd, not just read back. The supervisor reads both files itself and returned OK on each run.

Three things codex needed that Claude Code didn't
Multi-line prompts arrived truncated. The npm codex launcher is codex.cmd, and cmd.exe re-parses its arguments — the prompt was cut at the first newline. Codex concluded it had been asked for an empty file and wrote one, 0 bytes, then reported nothing. Prompts now go over stdin via codex's own - marker (runners.py:57-64).
It followed your repo conventions. Codex picked up the knowledge-base instructions and spent ~40s running maestro search (which isn't installed) before touching the file. Each prompt now has one clause telling it to skip that.
It wrote a BOM. Codex writes files through PowerShell on Windows, so Hi World! landed as \ufeffHi World! — the supervisor passed it because Read ignores a BOM, but the bytes were wrong. One added clause fixed it; both files are now exact.
One residue worth knowing: codex-written files get a trailing newline (\r\n), Claude Code's don't. I left that alone — it's normal text-file behaviour, not a defect. Say the word if you want them byte-identical.

Working now. Two separate breakages, neither of them in the logic.

What was wrong
1. The imports had been rewritten to a package that doesn't exist. After the folder was renamed yt-tutorial → yt_tutorial, something (IDE auto-import, most likely) rewrote seven lines across temp_agents/ to from yt_tutorial.config import …. There's no yt_tutorial package on sys.path — no __init__.py, and the repo root isn't on the path — so every entry point died on line 5:


ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'yt_tutorial'
Only temp_agents/ was hit; teams/, claude_agents/ and llm/ were untouched. Reverted to the bare from config import … that the rest of the project uses.

2. python main.py from inside the folder could never have worked. That puts temp_agents/ on sys.path instead of the project root, so both config and the temp_agents package are invisible. I'd only written it for python -m temp_agents.main from the parent. Since running the file directly is the natural thing to do, main.py:13-16 now puts the project root on the path first, and both forms work from anywhere.

One more, found on the way
With the imports fixed, Agent A worked and Agent B failed. That was config, not code — CODEX_MODEL had been set to gpt-4o:


The 'gpt-4o' model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account.
Set back to "" in config.py:168, which defers to whatever your codex config picks (gpt-5.6-sol on your machine). If you want to pin a model there, it has to be one your ChatGPT account is entitled to.

That failure was also much harder to read than it should have been: codex prints its banner before working and the real error last, so my stderr[:300] reported the workdir and sandbox settings instead of the reason. runners.py:129-147 now pulls the last ERROR: line and unwraps its JSON message.

Verified

python main.py          (from temp_agents/)   → both agents OK
file_A.txt  →  4865 6c6c 6f20 576f 726c 6421   "Hello World!"
file_B.txt  →  4869 2057 6f72 6c64 21          "Hi World!"
Supervisor read both files back and returned OK on each. python -m temp_agents.main and importing from the repo root both resolve too, and no .py file still references the old hyphenated path.

Worth knowing: your .venv survived the rename because a venv finds its prefix from the adjacent pyvenv.cfg, but the Activate.ps1 script has the old path baked in. If your prompt shows (.venv) while python silently does nothing, that's a stale activation falling through to the Windows Store stub — re-activate from the new path.

Set-Location 'C:\Users\LOQ\Desktop\Projects\Bhai-To-Bhai\yt_tutorial'; . .\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1; "VIRTUAL_ENV = $env:VIRTUAL_ENV"; "python -> " + (Get-Command python).Source; python --version; Set-Location .\temp_agents; "--- running: python .\main.py ---"; python .\main.py 2>&1 | Select-Object -Last 12

Your exact command works. The problem is the shell, not the code.

The silence explained
Your prompt says yt-tutorial; the folder is yt_tutorial now. That PowerShell session was activated before the rename, so its PATH still points at …\yt-tutorial\.venv\Scripts — a directory that no longer exists. python falls through to the Microsoft Store stub, which I measured:


C:\Users\LOQ\AppData\Local\Microsoft\WindowsApps\python.exe --version
  output:   (empty)
  exitcode: 9009
No output, prompt returns. Exactly what you saw — twice, because nothing about the shell changed between attempts. No Python ever ran, so none of my fixes could have taken effect.

Fix

cd C:\Users\LOQ\Desktop\Projects\Bhai-To-Bhai\yt_tutorial
.\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
cd temp_agents
python .\main.py
That's the run above verbatim — python resolved to …\yt_tutorial\.venv\Scripts\python.exe (3.12.10), and both agents came back OK with file_A.txt = Hello World! and file_B.txt = Hi World!.

Activate.ps1 derives its location from $PSScriptRoot, so it survived the rename. Two things didn't:

activate.bat has the old path hardcoded (line 29). Harmless in PowerShell, broken in cmd.exe. Recreate the venv if you use cmd.
Any shell opened before the rename is stuck the same way. Close them.
If you want to skip activation entirely, this always works regardless of PATH state:


..\.venv\Scripts\python.exe .\main.py
Worth guarding against
That failure mode is nasty because it's indistinguishable from "my program did nothing." If it bites again, Get-Command python | Select Source tells you in one line whether you're talking to the venv or the stub.

The code fixes from the last round — the yt_tutorial.config imports and the sys.path bootstrap that makes python main.py work from inside the folder — are what made this run succeed once real Python was reached. CODEX_MODEL is still back at ""; leave it there unless you pin a model your ChatGPT account can use.
